---
title: Version compatibility & changelog
---
Bynk is pre-1.0 and developed in small, spec-first increments (see
[Versioning & roadmap](/book/about/versioning-and-roadmap/)). This book is
written against **v0.404**.

This page is a high-level summary of notable increments, not an exhaustive
per-commit history. While Bynk is pre-1.0, increments may change behaviour.

> _Docs (under v0.163, no version change):_ the enum-spelling convention is now
> stated — a **payloadless** sum is canonically written `enum { A, B }` (the
> pipe form `| A | B` is for sums that carry a payload); `enum { … }` is exactly
> sugar for the payloadless pipe form, so the two are the same type. This closes
> the keyword-hygiene batch ([#548](https://github.com/accuser/bynk/issues/548)):
> no language change (both spellings remain valid), a docs convention only.

> _Docs & examples (under v0.78, no version change):_ the
> [`examples/` gallery](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/tree/main/examples) was
> refreshed — six of the seven projects now ship unit tests, with each one's pure
> logic (a refined type's boundary, a key helper, a windowing or health policy)
> factored into a tested `commons`. The gallery's testing note was corrected to
> reflect what is testable today (see
> [#291](https://github.com/accuser/bynk/issues/291) for the platform-capability
> test-surface limitation the split works around).

## Recent increments

| Version | Highlights |
|---|---|
| **v0.404.0** | "`bynk.cloudflare` gains `EdgeCache`: declaring `capability Edge = EdgeCache` turns Cloudflare's Workers Cache on for the context's Worker (`[cache] enabled = true`), `purge(prefixes) -> Effect[Bool]` purges it by path prefix, and an opted-in Worker sends `no-store` on every `GET`/`HEAD` answer but a `@cache` route's `2xx`, so a `404` is never cached; refused from agents (`bynk.cloudflare.edge_cache_in_agent`), queue consumers, `on schedule` (deployed, a cron run's purge clears nothing) and exported capabilities, at most one per context; a `@cache(scope: public)` route there must be served by an open actor, and a locale-negotiating Worker sends `Vary: accept-language` (first-party registry L7, [#2414](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2414))" |
| **v0.403.4** | "The first-party registry service moved to its own repository, [`bynk-lang/registry`](https://github.com/bynk-lang/registry), before going live (#2415): it builds, tests and deploys on the `bynk` release it pins, and a daily `registry-canary` workflow here runs it against `main`'s toolchain. Nothing in the toolchain changes" |
| **v0.403.3** | "A registry client refuses an index line whose `toolchain` or `withdrawn` is present but not a string (or, for `toolchain`, `null`) — a serialiser's tagged `Option` included — instead of reading it as absent, so `bynk registry conformance` fails such a registry rather than passing `read.withdrawn` for want of a withdrawn line" |
| **v0.403.2** | "The first-party registry service takes versions down (R5a.2, [#2397](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2397)): an operator token that can `withdraw` tombstones a version through `POST /_operator/withdraw/<org>/<package>/<version>`, deleting its archive from the store and the backup, refunding the quota and logging it; an `operate` token restores an organisation from the export; and index lines are written in the protocol's shape, so clients see `withdrawn`" |
| **v0.403.1** | 'Editor tooling and docs for `embed`: hover shows the declaration, that it is private, and its doc; completion offers embed names wherever they are in scope (a unit''s own and its `uses`d commons''); go-to-definition, references, rename and doc links reach them; embed names get their own `embed` semantic token and the VS Code extension an `embed` snippet; and a reference page and a guide cover embedded text (embedded text, slice 3)' |
| **v0.403.0** | "A raw-body `201` for HTTP handlers (first-party registry L6, [#2403](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2403)): `RawCreated(body, contentType)` answers `201` with author-owned bytes under its own `content-type` and no codec, as `Raw` does for `200`" |
| **v0.402.2** | A provider that calls an agent handler must list the handler's `given` capabilities, transitively, in its own `given` (`bynk.provider.agent_call_undeclared_capability`). Before, `bynk check` passed and `tsc` failed later (#2402) |
| **v0.402.1** | "`bynk fmt` lays a pipe-form sum type out one `| Variant` per line, indented under `type T =`" |
| **v0.402.0** | An `embed` is built: its file's text, read from the project's `embeds/` folder, is an exported `string` constant of the declaring module, with UTF-8, line-ending and size rules checked and a unit-tier `suite` case able to name it; a single-file build is `bynk.embed.needs_project` (embedded text, slice 2) |
| **v0.401.0** | A context or commons declares a project file's text with `embed name = "path"`, a `String` constant that is never served; declarations are checked, and a build refuses them with `bynk.embed.not_yet_emitted` until they can be emitted (embedded text, slice 1) |
| **v0.400.3** | "The first-party registry service has its deploy workflow (R5.3, [#2392](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2392)): `deploy-registry.yml` deploys a release tag on `main` only, with that release's checked `bynk` and admission module, to staging first (held to full conformance and a real OIDC exchange) and then, after an approval, to production" |
| **v0.400.2** | "The first-party registry service exports itself every day (R5.2, [#2391](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2391)): an hourly cron copies the bucket, each package's authoritative lines and each organisation to a backup bucket, `GET /_status/export` reports progress, and CI restores a fresh bucket from the backup alone" |
| **v0.400.1** | "The first-party registry service takes abuse limits (R5.1, [#2390](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2390)): writes are rate-limited per token, the account API per IP, the trusted-publishing exchange per repository, with a per-IP flood guard on writes; each organisation has a 1 GiB storage quota, raised by `REGISTRY_QUOTAS`; the protocol page lists the optional `429 rate_limited` and `413 quota_exceeded` refusals" |
| **v0.400.0** | Per-organisation publish tokens (first-party registry R3.2, [#2383](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2383)): `bynk publish` and `bynk yank` prefer the credentials file's `[registries.<name>.organisations.<org>] token` for the organisation they act for, falling back to `[registries.<name>] token`; `BYNK_REGISTRY_<NAME>_TOKEN` still overrides both; `bynk org token mint --save` writes the organisation's own slot; a `401` or `403` names where the refused token came from |
| **v0.399.1** | "The first-party registry service takes trusted publishing (R4, [#2381](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2381)): a GitHub Actions job exchanges its OIDC token for a 15-minute publish token, when the organisation's owner has trusted that repository (by its stable id), workflow file and, optionally, environment" |
| **v0.399.0** | "An `Oidc` actor's binder exposes the verified token's claims (first-party registry L5, [#2380](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2380)): `who.claims.get(\"repository\")`, a read-only `TokenClaims`; `trustFrom = \"<ENV>\"` overrides the actor's trust roots from the environment, and a test passes claims with `by Workload(\"id\", TokenClaims.of([…]))`" |
| **v0.398.0** | The account client (first-party registry R3.1, [#2378](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2378)): `bynk login` signs in to a registry's accounts through GitHub's device flow and keeps the account token in the credentials file's `[accounts.<name>]`; `bynk logout` signs out; `bynk org` claims, shows, blocks and unblocks an organisation, adds and removes members, and mints and revokes publish tokens, with `token mint --save` writing the new token where `bynk publish` reads it |
| **v0.397.7** | "`bynk publish` and `bynk yank` refuse a registry read through a mirror (`bynk.registry.publish_refused`), as the account commands do: the mirror's `config.json` names the publish API, so an upload and its token would go where the mirror says" |
| **v0.397.6** | "The tree-sitter grammar accepts `trace` and `advance` as ordinary identifiers outside `trace(Cap.op)` and `advance Clock by …`, matching the compiler — editor highlighting no longer breaks on a `let trace = …` binding" |
| **v0.397.5** | "The first-party registry service has accounts (R3, [#2363](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2363)): people sign in with GitHub's device flow, claim organisations, add members, block names, and mint expiring, revocable publish tokens; operator tokens remain for bootstrap and CI" |
| **v0.397.4** | "vscode-bynk Test Explorer: a refused step of a failed history adds a line naming the handler it called, opening that handler (Test Explorer depth track, Slice F follow-up)" |
| **v0.397.3** | "On the Workers target, requiring another context's capability whose provider constructs that context's agents is now a compile error (`bynk.capability.provider_agent_cross_context`), not a type error in `tsc` or a silent fallback to in-isolate agent state under `bynk dev`" |
| **v0.397.2** | "vscode-bynk Test Explorer: a failed test names the invariant or transition that refused it, and a failed history names the declaration that refused each step, each line opening the declaration (Test Explorer depth track, Slice F)" |
| **v0.397.1** | "The first-party registry service takes publishes (R2, [#2336](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2336)): `bynk publish` and `bynk yank` work against it, it signs with an online delegate it checks against `config.json`, and it passes the full `bynk registry conformance` run; `bynk-registry-core` now owns index lines' signing payload and rendering" |
| **v0.397.0** | "`on rejected` (first-party registry L4, [#2341](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2341)): a `from http` service can render the router's own `400`, `405`, `413` and `415` (and a `Signature` seam's `401`) in its contract's shape, as a `RawError` of the rejection's status" |
| **v0.396.0** | "Request headers and author-owned error bodies (first-party registry L4, [#2341](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2341)): an HTTP handler may take `headers: RequestHeaders` (`get`, `bearer`, `clientIp`), and `RawError(status, body, contentType)` answers a 4xx/5xx `ErrorStatus` with a body you choose; tests build headers with `RequestHeaders.of`" |
| **v0.395.2** | A failed history names the invariant or transition that refused each step in its shrunk sequence |
| **v0.395.1** | A context-local provider that calls one of its context's agents now reaches it — on `workers` the provider receives the Worker's `env` through its deps, where it used to fault with `ReferenceError: deps is not defined` |
| **v0.395.0** | "Withdrawn versions (first-party registry R5a.1, [#2330](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2330)): an index line may carry an unsigned `withdrawn` reason; the client never chooses a withdrawn version, and a locked one whose tree isn't in the store fails as `bynk.registry.withdrawn`; `bynk registry withdraw` performs the takedown; the publish API answers `410`/`409 withdrawn`; conformance gains `read.withdrawn`. Older toolchains see a withdrawn version as yanked, and fail on its fetch as `bynk.registry.unavailable`" |
| **v0.394.2** | A failed case names the invariant or transition it violated, with that declaration's kind and `path:line:col` |
| **v0.394.1** | A registry at `http://localhost` is reached on IPv4 first, so on Windows each request no longer waits about two seconds for `::1` to refuse it |
| **v0.394.0** | "`bynk.cloudflare` gains `RateLimit`, Cloudflare's Rate Limiting binding as an instance-only derived capability (`capability PublishLimit = RateLimit @limit(20) @period(60.seconds)`) with one operation, `allow(key) -> Effect[Bool]`; `bynk deploy` allocates each limiter's `namespace_id` in the ledger; per-instance test doubles count on the harness clock (standard-library P3, [#2342](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2342))" |
| **v0.393.2** | The first-party registry service begins (#2308, R1): a Bynk project at `services/registry/` serves the registry protocol's read paths — `config.json`, index files and archives — from an R2 bucket owned by one context, seeded from a `bynk registry` directory built and signed offline, and held to the protocol in CI by `bynk registry conformance --read-only` against `bynk dev`. Nothing in the toolchain changes |
| **v0.393.1** | A property that violates an invariant shrinks to a counterexample and reports its seed, and a failed property or history links to the failing `expect` |
| **v0.393.0** | "Caching raw responses (first-party registry L3, [#2335](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2335)): a `Raw` `GET` body of at most 1 MiB gets the automatic weak `ETag` and `304`, the new `RawTagged(body, contentType, etag)` variant sends a handler-supplied `ETag`, and `@cache(…, immutable: true)` appends `immutable` to `Cache-Control`" |
| **v0.392.0** | `bynk.crypto` (experimental) — effectful Web Crypto digests, HMAC, constant-time comparison and sign/verify with non-storable key handles, and `Random.bytes(n)` (standard-library K4) |
| **v0.391.1** | The registry's admission rules move into the I/O-free `bynk-registry-core` crate, which `bynk registry add` and `serve` link and which also builds to a standalone wasm32 module |
| **v0.391.0** | "Raw HTTP request bodies (first-party registry L1, [#2320](https://github.com/bynk-lang/bynk/issues/2320)): `@raw(contentType:)` takes a handler's `body: Bytes` as the octets sent, a `415` answers another `Content-Type`, a raw route must have a body cap, and every capped body (JSON too) is now counted as it is read" |
| **v0.390.0** | "`bynk.cloudflare` gains `R2`, object storage as `Bytes` objects, used only through derived instances (`capability Archives = R2`), with metadata, conditional writes, paged listing, per-instance test doubles and ledger-provisioned buckets (#2323)" |
| **v0.389.1** | "vscode-bynk Test Explorer: a Watch toggle reruns the watched tests when a `.bynk` file or `bynk.toml` changes, and Run with Coverage on a single case measures that case alone, named for it (Test Explorer depth track, Slice E)" |
| **v0.389.0** | An adapter `binding` can `includes [ "./x.wasm" ]` a WebAssembly module, loaded through a compiler-generated shim and `loadWasm`, on Workers and under `bynk test` |
| **v0.388.1** | "`bynk test --format ndjson` reports each case as it finishes, with its `durationMs`, and `suite-begin.tests` is known before the suite runs; the Test Explorer shows results case by case with durations (Test Explorer depth track, Slice D stage 2)" |
| **v0.388.0** | First-party registry R5b — registry key delegation and revocation. A registry's chain key can delegate line signing to another key and later revoke it (`delegations` and `revoked` in `config.json`, which then speaks protocol 2), and a line's signature may be re-signed. Clients accept lines signed by the chain or a valid, unrevoked delegate, refuse a revoked delegate's lines naming the revocation, and ignore (and report) a delegation or revocation no chain key signed. A leaked delegate is replaced without clients re-pinning. New `bynk registry rotate`, `delegate`, `revoke` and `resign`; `serve` and `add` sign with a delegate; `conformance` gains `config.delegations`, `publish.signer` and `read.revoked` |
| **v0.387.1** | The VS Code Test Explorer runs and debugs a multi-item selection as one `bynk test` process with the repeatable `--case` and `--suite`, instead of one process per case or the whole project (Test Explorer depth track, Slice A follow-up) |
| **v0.387.0** | First-party registry R0 — `bynk registry conformance <index-url>` checks any registry against the protocol, from `config.json` and its key chain through publish, read-back, refusals, auth, the upload cap, yank and a fresh client's resolution, each check citing the protocol section it holds the registry to (`bynk.registry.nonconformant` on a failure; `--format json` for CI; `--read-only` publishes nothing). A publish retried with the same tree is now idempotent — `200` with the existing line, where `bynk registry serve` used to answer `409` — and `bynk registry add` of an identical package changes nothing |
| **v0.386.3** | "`bynk test --format ndjson` streams the run as one pinned JSON event per line, each suite as it finishes, and the VS Code Test Explorer shows results suite by suite as they arrive (Test Explorer depth track, Slice D stage 1)" |
| **v0.386.2** | "A WebSocket `on open` that transfers its connection to an agent in another file of a split context is now a compile error on the Workers target (`bynk.ws.open_transfer_cross_file`), not a silent `404`" |
| **v0.386.1** | "`bynk test` takes a repeatable `--case` and a new `--suite`, so one process runs a chosen set of tests, and a `--case` or `--suite` value that selects nothing now fails the run instead of passing with nothing run" |
| **v0.386.0** | Packaging slice 5 — approving a dependency's npm packages. An npm package that a git or registry dependency's adapter requires enters the build only once the project approves it in `bynk.lock` (`approved = true` on its `[[npm]]` row); until then a build reaching the adapter fails with `bynk.requires.unapproved`, naming the chain that brought it in. `bynk approve` reviews and approves (`<package>`, `--all`, `--list`), and `bynk add`, `fetch` and `update` offer it on a terminal. An approval is one package at one range, so a changed range asks again; path dependencies and the project's own adapters need none. **Upgrading:** a lock written before this has no approvals, so run `bynk approve` (or `bynk approve --all`) once |
| **v0.385.3** | "`bynkc test --coverage` anchors a WebSocket-hosted lifecycle method's header, and the agent's dispatch code after it, to the right `.bynk` lines" |
| **v0.385.2** | The type-system spec names its primitives `Float` and `Instant`, as the compiler does, in place of `Decimal` and `Timestamp` |
| **v0.385.1** | The VS Code Test Explorer shows a failed property's seed and counterexample as data, re-runs it with that seed, and can pin the seed per test (held back when the suite's property tests change); `bynk test --format json` gives a failed property or history property `kind`, `seed` and `counterexample`, and `--no-run` marks generative cases with `kind` |
| **v0.385.0** | Packaging slice 4c — publishing over HTTP. A registry's `config.json` may name a publish `api`; `bynk publish` checks and packs a package, uploads it there with the registry's token, and verifies the line the registry signed by reading it back, and `bynk yank` yanks or un-yanks through the same API (`bynk.registry.publish_refused` carries a refusal's reason). `bynk registry serve` puts the API in front of a registry directory, signing uploads with a key publishers never hold and authorising them with hashed tokens from `bynk registry token`, scoped to organisations and to `publish` or `yank` |
| **v0.384.1** | "`bynkc test --coverage` anchors a provider op's header, and the code after the last op, to the right `.bynk` lines" |
| **v0.384.0** | "`bynk fmt` always breaks braced declaration bodies: record types, `fn`/handler bodies and multi-field record literals go one item per line, however short; `match` arms and `if` branches stay compact; a `suite` header gets a blank line and `case`/`property` keep their order" |
| **v0.383.3** | The VS Code Test Explorer tags each test with its tier (`unit`, `integration`, `system`) and adds **Run against…**, which runs the `system` tests against `dev` or a `[test.targets]` entry; `bynk test --format json` gains an optional per-case `tier` |
| **v0.383.2** | "`bynk migrate sweep` no longer raises a class's floor until each swept Worker's code was deployed at least 30 minutes earlier, because Cloudflare's objects listing can lag a newly stored instance by about 10 minutes. A run inside that window still migrates, and says how long to wait. A rename an earlier deploy already applied now reads as applied in later `bynk deploy` plans." |
| **v0.383.1** | The VS Code Test Explorer runs and reports exactly the tests you select (a suite, several tests, or all but an excluded few), shows a test a remote run skipped as skipped rather than failed, debugs the selected test, links a suite to its file, and adds **Bynk: Run Tests in This File** |
| **v0.383.0** | Packaging slice 4a — registries. A `{ package, version }` dependency resolves on the registry its organisation routes to in `[registries]`, at the highest version every semver range admits; `bynk` verifies each index line's ed25519 signature (bound to the registry `id` and the package name) against a pinned key or one trusted on first use and recorded in `bynk.lock`, follows key rotation, fetches the archive into the store only when its tree checksum is the signed one, and refuses a misrouted organisation (`bynk.registry.source_mismatch`). Tokens go only to the registry's own origin. `bynk add` writes a registry dependency; `bynk registry init`/`add`/`yank` build a static registry any file host can serve, or that a project reads as `file://`. The first-party registry isn't live yet |
| **v0.382.2** | "`bynkc test --coverage` anchors a service handler's header, and the code after the last handler, to the right `.bynk` lines" |
| **v0.382.1** | "`bynkc test --coverage` no longer reports an executed line as uncovered next to an uncalled agent handler" |
| **v0.382.0** | Packaging slice 3 — a dependency's adapter can wrap an npm library. When a project reaches that adapter, its `binding … requires { … }` packages install with the project's own in the generated `package.json` (pinned ranges only; two adapters, in any packages, requiring one npm package at different ranges is `bynk.requires.conflicting_range`, naming both as `org/package:adapter`), and `bynk.lock` gains `[[npm]]` rows recording every npm package a dependency may bring in and the adapters requiring it, so `--locked` stops a build when a dependency adds one |
| **v0.381.1** | A cross-context call whose callee faulted is reported as `CalleeFaulted`, naming the callee's context and service, instead of `Transport`; the callee's `500` carries `X-Bynk-Fault`, and the caller logs `CalleeFaulted <caller> -> <callee> call <service>` before it faults in turn (#2193) |
| **v0.381.0** | "`bynk migrate status` shows each agent class's version, floor, migrate steps and the versions they cover, and where it is deployed. `bynk migrate sweep` wakes every stored agent instance in the deployed Workers so each migrates, then raises a class's floor when one run covered every environment it is deployed to; a step below the floor warns `bynk.agent.migrate_below_floor` and can be deleted." |
| **v0.380.1** | An HTTP route whose path parameter is a refined or opaque type from a consumed context no longer faults with a `500` on every request; the entry validates it with the context's own codec for that type, so a bad value gets the usual `400` `RefinementViolation` (#2252) |
| **v0.380.0** | An agent class can now be renamed with `agent New replaces Old` (its stored data and schema history move) or removed with `removed agent Old` (its stored data is deleted). Each emits a Cloudflare `exports` tombstone. `bynk deploy` plans both, and refuses a deletion until each class is confirmed by name (`--confirm-delete <context>.<Class>`); `--yes` alone never deletes. New diagnostics: `bynk.agent.replaces_unknown`, `replaces_live`, `removed_live`, `class_conflict`. |
| **v0.379.3** | The release check on the extension's pin also requires the `bynk` and `bynkc` binaries from `v0.379.2` on |
| **v0.379.2** | The VS Code extension downloads `bynk` and `bynkc` beside the language server, so no `cargo install` is needed to check, test, run or deploy |
| **v0.379.1** | A Worker now reports each fault it catches to Cloudflare as well as logging it, so with `--upload-source-maps` the exception `wrangler tail` shows is mapped to `.bynk` lines; the `500` and a queue message's per-message `retry()` are unchanged, and `fetch` and `queue` take an optional `ctx` (#2192) |
| **v0.379.0** | Packaging slice 2b — git dependencies (`{ package, git, rev }`) are fetched by the user's own hardened `git` into a per-user content-addressed store, and `bynk.lock` pins each to a commit and a sha256 checksum of its files; `bynk fetch` and `bynk update` are new, `--locked` and `--offline` make `check`, `test`, `dev` and `deploy` refuse a lock change or a fetch, a workspace shares one lock, and `[dev-dependencies]` resolve for suites only and never reach a deployable build. `bynkc` and the editor read the lock and the store and never fetch |
| **v0.378.0** | An agent can declare `migrate from N (old: T) { … }` steps that carry a stored record from schema version N to its current shape. A step makes a non-additive `store` change buildable, runs when an older record loads (covering older versions whose path to N was additive too), and faults as `MigrationFailed` if it throws. New diagnostics: `bynk.agent.migrate_shape_mismatch`, `migrate_unknown_version`, `migrate_duplicate`, `migrate_duplicate_assignment`, `migrate_incomplete`, `migrate_impure`, and the `version_without_path` warning. |
| **v0.377.4** | `bynkc-lsp` exits after `exit` even if the client sends another message; it used to stay running for good (#2239) |
| **v0.377.3** | `bynkc test` now heads a `system` suite's results `system · <suite>:` rather than `integration · <suite>:`; suites still run in heading order, so in a project with unit suites too a `system` suite can now sort among them differently, in the human output and in the `--format json` `suites` array (#2185) |
| **v0.377.2** | The Workers guide no longer says an uploaded source map maps a fault's logged stack: Cloudflare applies an uploaded map only to an uncaught exception, and a Worker catches every fault to answer the `500`, so the frames in `wrangler tail` stay `index.js:L:C`; it points to `bynk dev --inspect`, pausing on caught exceptions, to see a fault at its `.bynk` line, and corrects the emitter's comments to match (#2192). |
| **v0.377.1** | The editor knows a project's path dependencies — after `uses` and `consumes`, completion offers a dependency's exported commons and adapters as `<alias>.<unit>` (never their bare names), a dependency adapter's capabilities complete inside `consumes <alias>.<unit> { … }` and in `given`, hover on a `<alias>.<unit>` target shows the unit's module doc and the package it comes from, and go-to-definition opens the dependency's source file |
| **v0.377.0** | The checker warns (`bynk.transition.may_violate`) when a handler can make a move an agent's `transition` refuses at commit, from a state the agent can be in after a commit — at the write, naming the path's guards, with the `transition` and the handler that reaches the start state as related information |
| **v0.376.0** | Packaging slice 2a — path dependencies work end to end. A project declares `[dependencies] money = { package = "acme/money", path = "…" }` and reaches the package's exported commons and adapters as `money.currency`; `bynk check`, `test`, `dev`, `deploy` and the editor build the same graph, a dependency's units are emitted under `out/deps/<org>/<package>/`, and only the units a project reaches are compiled. Git and registry sources, `[dev-dependencies]`, and a dependency adapter's npm `requires` are refused with the slice that brings them |
| **v0.375.3** | The agent state model behind the editor's state view now lives in `bynk-check`, shared with the checker, and no longer mistakes a local `let`, match binding or lambda parameter named like a store field for that field when deciding whether a handler commits |
| **v0.375.2** | Two adapters that `requires` one npm package at different ranges are refused with `bynk.requires.conflicting_range` instead of the last range silently winning in `package.json` (#2201) |
| **v0.375.1** | The agent state view no longer flags a move a `transition` would refuse when the agent can only make that move on its first commit, which the runtime never checks |
| **v0.375.0** | An agent built with `bynk.schema.lock` now stamps every stored record with its schema version (`__v`). A record from a newer build, after a rollback, faults on load as `RehydrationViolation`/`NewerSchema` and is never written back, and a malformed marker faults with `NoMigrationPath`. Every agent logs, once per isolate and by name only, a stored field its definition no longer declares. |
| **v0.374.0** | Packaging slice 1 — `bynk.toml` gains `[package]` (an `organisation/package` identity, with `exports` checked by `bynk check`), `[dependencies]` and `[dev-dependencies]` (checked, then refused until resolution ships), `[registries]` (checked configuration) and `[workspace]`, whose root runs `bynk check`, `fmt` and `test` across its members |
| **v0.373.0** | **Lexical breaking change.** A comment is `//` and a one-line doc is `--- text` (#2203). `--` and `--|` are retired: each is now an error with a machine-applicable fix (`bynk.lex.legacy_comment`, `bynk.lex.legacy_doc_line`), `///` points to `---` (`bynk.lex.slash_doc`), and two docs on one target is `bynk.parse.multiple_docs`. `a--b` is subtraction. Run `bynk fmt` over a project to migrate it. |
| **v0.372.2** | The agent state view shows each handler's effects — what it writes, reads and calls on each path, and what it yields — in place of a state machine for an agent with no enum state field, and collapsed under the machine for the rest |
| **v0.372.1** | The agent state view flags, as you edit, every move a `transition` step invariant would refuse at commit — red when it always would, amber when a computed write only may — and lists the field's `transition`s it can't evaluate |
| **v0.372.0** | `bynk.schema.lock` now records each agent's stored state shape, and a change deployed data cannot be read through (a removed, renamed, retyped or re-kinded `store` field, or a removed agent) fails `bynkc check`, `bynkc test` and the build with `bynk.agent.non_additive_state_change` or `bynk.agent.class_removed`; a changed refinement warns (`bynk.agent.refinement_changed`). The lock moves to `version = 2`: a build rewrites a v1 lock once, and an older Bynk refuses a v2 one. |
| **v0.371.8** | The VS Code extension's **Show Agent State** view (and a `Show State` CodeLens on each agent) draws an agent's state machines from its handlers — a state diagram and a handler × state table of what each handler does from each state — live, click-to-code, following the cursor from agent to agent |
| **v0.371.7** | The IDE crate derives each agent's state machine from its handlers (`bynk_ide::agent_state::agent_state_model`) — the model the coming state view renders; no user-visible change yet |
| **v0.371.6** | The VS Code sequence diagram shows an agent handler's writes to its own state — each `:=` and mutating store operation (`status := Placed`, `items.put(id, …)`) — as notes in source order inside their branch, click-to-code |
| **v0.371.5** | The VS Code extension highlights the Markdown in doc comments (`---` doc-blocks and `--|` lines) in the editor, including ```bynk examples as Bynk; a doc-block still closes on its own marker |
| **v0.371.4** | The VS Code architecture map renders under themes whose colours contain commas (e.g. `rgba(…)`), and its expand/reveal clicks work again with Mermaid 12.2 |
| **v0.371.3** | Release-only bump after the v0.371.2 publish failed (the new `bynk-std` crate and `tree-sitter-bynk` need a token bootstrap first, and the bootstrap's server-pin check was stale); no language change |
| **v0.371.2** | "Hover and completion on `run.id` see an `is` test's bindings where the checker does: on the right of `&&` and `implies`, and in an `if`'s `else` block for a negated test. `bynk.test.remote_clock_dependent` no longer warns when a `match` arm, a lambda parameter or an `is` binding rebinds a constant's name to a run value (#2181)" |
| **v0.371.1** | "`run.id` is fresh for every `bynk test --against` run, even with `BYNK_TEST_RUN_ID` left in the environment. `bynk.test.remote_clock_dependent` now follows run-derived instants through calls and checks every `expect` in a case, including those in `if` and `match` arms and after a `let … else`. Hover and completion on `run.id` respect every binder that shadows `run` (#2175)" |
| **v0.371.0** | A test case reads `run.id`, the run's identity — `"local"` in process, fresh for each `bynk test --against` run — so data a case creates on a shared deployment doesn't collide with an earlier run's. Under `--against`, an `expect` comparing an `Instant` built from a constant warns (`bynk.test.remote_clock_dependent`). New guide, *Testing against a running system* (#2138) |
| **v0.370.0** | Under `bynk test --against`, a failing `expect` is retried until it holds or `--settle` expires (default `5s`, `0` for none). Before each retry, the runner re-runs the addresses since the last unsafe one (anything but `GET`, `HEAD` or `OPTIONS`); an unsafe address never runs twice. `--format json` reports each case's `retries` (#2138) |
| **v0.369.0** | `bynk test --against dev` runs the `system` suites, unchanged, against a local `bynk dev` it starts itself, one per distinct `env`. It applies each `env` and the declared secrets as `-- --var`, waits for the `--ready-json` file, accepts only loopback URLs, and stops each session by its own pid. `bynkc test --against dev` is refused, because only `bynk` can start `bynk dev` (#2138) |
| **v0.368.0** | On an actor-sum route, the `system` driver now sends the credential of the member the case acts as (a token for a Bearer member, a signature for a `Signature` member, nothing for `Visitor`), instead of the primary member's. A `system` case can now call a sum route as any member, and `by Nobody`, `by Forged` and `by Stale` work on a sum with a `Signature` member. `bynk.test.signature_needs_in_process` is retired, and a forged signature is now made with a secret derived from the real one (#2155) |
| **v0.367.0** | `bynk test --against <name>` runs the `system` suites, unchanged, against a deployment named under `[test.targets]` in `bynk.toml`. Each Worker's URL comes from the target, and its secrets come from the environment. A bare URL, an unknown name, a `production = true` target and a missing secret are refused before any request. A case a real runtime can't honour, or whose `env` disagrees with the target's `vars`, is refused with a `bynk.test.remote_unsupported_*` diagnostic, or skipped under `--skip-unsupported`. `--format json` gains `transport`, plus `skipped` when a case is skipped (#2138) |
| **v0.366.0** | `bynk dev --ready-json FILE` writes `{ "<worker>": "<url>" }` to `FILE` once every served context answers an HTTP probe, so a test runner or CI script waits for the file instead of looping on a warm-up request; the file is removed when the session starts and when it stops (#2138) |
| **v0.365.0** | A `system`-tier case can now call a route whose actor is a `Signature` (HMAC webhook) actor. The driver signs each call as the actor declares, over exactly the bytes it sends, with the suite's `env` entry for the secret or a fixed test secret, and the Worker's real seam verifies it. `by Nobody` now also works on such a route, and the new reserved principals `by Forged` (a wrong secret) and `by Stale` (`tolerance + 60` seconds old) drive the seam to `Rejected(Unauthorized)`. New diagnostics `bynk.test.forged_needs_signature_route`, `bynk.test.forged_needs_system` and `bynk.test.stale_needs_timestamp`; `bynk.test.signature_needs_in_process` now covers only a `Signature` member of an actor sum (#2135) |
| **v0.364.0** | A suite or case can give its participants variables and secrets with `env { NAME = "value" … }`. At `system` the entries go on each participant's Worker `env`; at `unit`/`integration` they are what the `Secrets` double answers. A case's `env` layers over its suite's, and an entry naming a `Bearer` actor's secret is the one the `system` driver signs with. New diagnostics `bynk.suite.duplicate_env`, `bynk.suite.env_not_literal` and `bynk.suite.env_binding_clash` (#2134) |
| **v0.363.0** | A test case moves a harness clock with `advance Clock by <Duration>`. Each case's clock starts at the epoch, the `Clock` double reads it at `unit` and `integration`, and at `system` every participant's first-party `Clock` now reads it instead of wall-clock time, so a time-dependent case promotes unchanged. New diagnostics `bynk.test.advance_outside_case`, `bynk.test.advance_not_clock` and `bynk.test.advance_not_duration` (#2137) |
| **v0.362.0** | A `system`-tier case runs a cron handler with `svc.schedule(…)` through the Worker's real `scheduled` entry and delivers a queue message with `q.message(m)` through its real `queue` entry, after a JSON round trip; each address yields the handler's own result, and a queue-only target now qualifies for `system`. `bynk.test.schedule_needs_in_process` and `bynk.test.message_needs_in_process` are retired, and the drivers of one `system` case now share its harness, so a later call reads what an earlier one wrote (#2136) |
| **v0.361.3** | In vscode.dev and github.dev, the VS Code extension now analyses each workspace folder that has a `bynk.toml` as a whole project. It reads the folder through `vscode.workspace.fs` and runs the new `bynk-wasm` entry points `bynk_analyze_project`, `bynk_hover_project` and `bynk_complete_project`, which use `bynk_emit::project::analyse_in_memory_project`. A `uses` of a sibling unit resolves, every file's diagnostics are published, and the status bar reads **Bynk (web)**. A file outside any project is still analysed on its own (#2102). |
| **v0.361.2** | Extract-function re-checks its edit before offering it, refuses a selection containing `?`, and says why a capability-using selection can't be extracted |
| **v0.361.1** | A `system`-tier case that calls a route whose actor is `Oidc`-secured is now `bynk.test.oidc_needs_in_process`, instead of passing check and sending a request with no token that the seam can only refuse; the `system` driver has no `Oidc` token issuer yet, so call such a route from a `unit` or `integration` case |
| **v0.361.0** | '`bynkc compile` always writes `out/package.json` declaring `"type": "module"` (beside any adapter npm dependencies), so `tsc -p out/tsconfig.json` with emit on builds the output as ES modules rather than CommonJS: a declaration named `require` or (under TypeScript 7) `Object` no longer fails with TS2441, and a hand-written binding is checked under the ES-module rules Node runs it with, so an extensionless relative import is a `tsc` error (TS2835) instead of a run-time `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` (#2148)' |
| **v0.360.1** | A `system`-tier case that calls a route whose actor is `Signature`-secured is now `bynk.test.signature_needs_in_process`, instead of passing check and sending an unsigned request the seam can only refuse; the `system` driver has no `Signature` signer yet, so call such a route from a `unit` or `integration` case |
| **v0.360.0** | The language server offers a generate-provider-stub action that scaffolds `provides Cap = CapImpl { … }` for a capability nothing in its context provides, with each operation's body a typed default |
| **v0.359.2** | '`bynkc test` compiles the emitted TypeScript to ES modules, so a declaration named `Object` or `require` no longer fails `tsc` with TS2441 ("Compiler reserves name … in top level scope of a module"); `tsc` had been down-levelling the output to CommonJS, whose transform reserves those names (#2146)' |
| **v0.359.1** | The VS Code extension runs in vscode.dev and github.dev. A `browser` entry starts a web-worker language server over `bynk-wasm`'s `bynk_analyze`, `bynk_hover` and `bynk_complete`, giving diagnostics, hover and completion for each open `.bynk` file on its own (status bar **Bynk (web, single-file)**). Node-only commands carry `!isWeb`. The three wasm entry points take an optional platform name, and the web worker passes `cloudflare`, as `bynkc-lsp` analyses (#2102). |
| **v0.359.0** | The language server offers an inline-variable refactor (`refactor.inline`) that replaces a pure `let` binding's uses with its initialiser and deletes the `let` |
| **v0.358.1** | The VS Code project sidebar and live model views catch up with a slow analysis round after a save or an external change. When no diagnostics arrive to signal the round, they re-check on a backing-off schedule for about 30 s. A file open in the editor that changes on disk now also counts as an external change |
| **v0.358.0** | "`bynk test --coverage --format json` lists each file's `covered_lines` beside `uncovered`, and keeps paths project-relative under `bynk test .`; VS Code's Testing view gains **Run with Coverage**" |
| **v0.357.0** | The language server offers a fill-match-arms quick fix for a non-exhaustive `match`, inserting each missing arm with a typed default body, and resolves its edit lazily through `codeAction/resolve` |
| **v0.356.3** | "VS Code: **Bynk: Doctor** shows the `bynk doctor --format json` readiness checklist with one-click remedies, and test, debug and dev-server failures on a missing tool offer **Run Doctor**" |
| **v0.356.2** | The VS Code extension has a Bynk project sidebar. An activity-bar view lists the project's contexts, commons, adapters and tests, each expanding to its `consumes` edges in both directions and its declarations, every node click-to-source, with inline sequence-diagram and wire-contract actions on handlers. It is built from `bynk/architectureModel` and refreshes on the `bynk.views.refresh` trigger |
| **v0.356.1** | "VS Code: **Bynk: Deploy…** shows the `bynk deploy --dry-run` plan for a chosen environment and scope, then runs `bynk deploy` in a terminal with the driver's own confirmation; **Bynk: Show Deploy Plan** previews only" |
| **v0.356.0** | `bynk.toml` gets editor support from a JSON Schema at `https://bynk-lang.org/schemas/bynk.toml.json`, generated from the manifest's Rust types by `cargo xtask manifest-schema` and associated with `bynk.toml` by the VS Code extension, so a schema-aware TOML extension completes, documents and validates every table and key |
| **v0.355.0** | The language server offers a fix-all source action (`source.fixAll.bynk`) that applies every machine-applicable diagnostic fix in a file, runnable on save through `editor.codeActionsOnSave` |
| **v0.354.0** | "`bynk dev --format json` writes one lifecycle event per line (`serving` with each context's ports, `rebuilding`, `rebuild_ok`, `rebuild_failed`, `stopped`); VS Code gains **Bynk: Start/Stop/Restart Dev Server**, a `bynk: dev` task and a status-bar item with each context's URL" |
| **v0.353.4** | A `system`-tier case that runs a cron handler with `svc.schedule(…)` or delivers a queue message with `svc.message(…)` is now `bynk.test.schedule_needs_in_process` or `bynk.test.message_needs_in_process`, even when the target also exposes an `http` service, instead of passing check and emitting TypeScript that `tsc` rejects; run such a case `as integration` |
| **v0.353.3** | The VS Code model views (sequence diagram, documentation, architecture map, wire contract) are live. They refresh on save (`bynk.views.refresh`: `onSave`, `onType` or `manual`), the sequence and wire-contract views follow the cursor between handlers unless pinned, open views are restored after a window reload, and each view has an editor title-bar button and an opt-in `Wire contract` handler CodeLens (`bynk.viewsCodeLens`) |
| **v0.353.2** | `bynk test --inspect` takes a free inspector port when Node's default 9229 is already in use, instead of running the tests with no inspector; the VS Code extension's Debug Tests no longer fails when another process holds 9229 |
| **v0.353.1** | Renaming or moving a folder of `.bynk` files in the editor now updates the units it holds and every `uses`/`consumes` reference to them, as a single-file rename already did; a move into another project is left alone |
| **v0.353.0** | The language server offers an organise `uses`/`consumes` source action (`source.organizeImports`), runnable on save through `editor.codeActionsOnSave` |
| **v0.352.2** | 'VS Code: the `bynk.testCodeLens` setting now says `Run Test` and `Debug Test` each target just that case (`bynk test --case`); the `messages` semantic token gets a theme fallback (`entity.name.type.struct`) and the README lists all seven token types; and new snippets cover `asset` declarations (with and without `at`) and tiered suites and cases (`suite … as unit|integration|system`, `case … as …`) (#2090, #2091, #2092)' |
| **v0.352.1** | The playground converts between editor (UTF-16) and compiler (UTF-8 byte) offsets, so hover, completion and diagnostic squiggles stay in place after non-ASCII text |
| **v0.352.0** | A diagnostic with a curated explanation offers an **Explain** quick fix in VS Code that shows the installed compiler's own explanation in a panel, offline, served by the new `bynk/explain` language-server request; **Bynk: Explain Diagnostic Code…** looks up any code |
| **v0.351.2** | A Dev Container feature, `ghcr.io/bynk-lang/features/bynk`, installs `bynk`, `bynkc` and `bynkc-lsp` from one checksum-verified release plus `wrangler@4` and TypeScript, and the repo ships a `.devcontainer/` for contributors |
| **v0.351.1** | The VS Code extension highlights `bynk` fenced code blocks (backtick or tilde fences) in Markdown files with the Bynk grammar; editor only, the built-in Markdown preview is unchanged |
| **v0.351.0** | `Bytes` gains `concat`, `slice` (clamped like `String.slice`), `compare` (unsigned lexicographic, `-1`/`0`/`1`), `toHex` and `toBase64Url`, plus the partial statics `Bytes.fromHex` and `Bytes.fromBase64Url`. `Bytes` stays unorderable |
| **v0.350.1** | A value of a consumed context's record whose field is an unexported opaque type that another context also declares by name now crosses a consumed service's boundary, instead of being refused with `structural_mismatch` |
| **v0.350.0** | A call to a consumed context's service whose signature names a type that context consumes (`isDomestic(o: Order)`, `Order` from a third context) now checks and runs for every caller, `system` suites included. A caller must consume every context that a called service's signature types reach (`bynk.consumes.signature_type_unconsumed`); this also refuses a call such as `shop.order.place`, returning `shop.order`'s own `Order` with a `shop.base` field, from a caller that does not consume `shop.base`. That call compiled on `bundle` but failed `tsc` on `workers`. The `X-Bynk-Contract` hash of every service whose signature names a consumed context's type now describes that type's shape, so it moves for services that already worked; redeploy both Workers of such a pair together, or the call answers `ContractMismatch` |
| **v0.349.4** | On the bundle target, a cross-context argument at a generic parameter that names a user type, such as `List[Line]` or `Map[String, Line]`, is cast to the callee's type, so a caller's own commons values pass `tsc` |
| **v0.349.3** | On `workers`, a cross-context call argument of a named type is cast to the callee's type, so a caller's own commons values built into it, such as `Map.empty()` in a field of a consumed record, pass `tsc` |
| **v0.349.2** | A test case's `Json.encode`/`Json.decode[T]` on a consumed context's type now runs, its generated codecs naming the type through that context, instead of failing `tsc` |
| **v0.349.1** | A test case that names a consumed context's nullary variant bare (`Domestic` for `Region.Domestic`) now runs, as the same name does in a handler, instead of failing `tsc` |
| **v0.349.0** | A test case delivers an event to a `from Events(E)` service with `svc.event(e)` or `svc.event(e, env)`, honouring its pattern filter and `via schema(N)` guard |
| **v0.348.1** | A literal set on a consumed context's refined field (`Item { code: 1, … }` for `ev.store`'s `Code`) is now cast to `ev_store.Code` under the namespace the module already imports, instead of a bare `Code` it never imported, which failed `tsc` with `TS2304` |
| **v0.348.0** | A refined literal in a field of a generic record passed to a cross-context call (`Page { items: [250] }` for a `Page[Cents]` parameter), or in a record literal that also has a shorthand field, is admitted against the callee's own declaration, as other nested literals are, so it crosses from a caller with its own type of that name; a shorthand field is checked by its binding's type |
| **v0.347.1** | On `workers`, a context imports a consumed context's types type-only (`import { type Receipt }`) and builds that context's sum variants inline (`({ tag: "Held", reason } as Status)`), so the callee's module stays out of the caller's Worker bundle; a consumed type with attached methods still imports as a value |
| **v0.347.0** | A refined literal nested in a cross-context argument, in an `Option`/`Result` payload, a list element or a record field (`t.pay.maybe(Some(250))`), is admitted against the callee's own declaration, as a direct literal is, so it crosses from a caller with no `uses` of the declaring commons or with its own type of that name; an empty `[]`, `None`, `List.empty()` or `Map.empty()` passed positionally takes its parameter's type and crosses from such a caller too |
| **v0.346.12** | The editor now reports a non-additive event change against the committed `bynk.schema.lock` as `bynk.event.non_additive_schema_change`, as `bynk check` does: the language server reads the lock read-only, caches it by its modification stamp, re-reads it when it changes (a new `**/bynk.schema.lock` file watcher), and passes it to `bynk_ide::diagnose_project_with`, whose new `schema_lock` argument `bynk_check::analysis::analyse_project` now also takes (#2081). |
| **v0.346.11** | The formatter's comment-slot test pins its refusal counts per fixture, in `bynk-fmt/tests/comment_slots/`, so adding a fixture adds its own pin file instead of editing one shared count |
| **v0.346.10** | A non-additive event change now fails `bynkc check` and `bynkc test` (and `bynk check`/`bynk test`) with `bynk.event.non_additive_schema_change`, as it already failed `bynkc compile`, `bynk dev` and `bynk deploy`: both reconcile events against the committed `bynk.schema.lock` read-only and never write it, so an additive change passes and the next build records it (#2078). |
| **v0.346.9** | A new Worker's first `bynk deploy` now uploads its secrets with its code (`wrangler deploy --secrets-file`, from a 0600 temporary file deleted when the upload ends, even if `bynk` gets Ctrl-C or SIGTERM, with a SIGKILLed run's file removed by the next deploy), so the Worker is never live without them; it used to set them about four seconds after the upload. A redeploy still sets them first with `wrangler secret put`. Each secret set now prints a `bynk: set secret` line naming it and the Worker (`api-staging` under `--env staging`), never its value, beside the "already set, skipping" line, and a wrangler older than 4.74.0 falls back to the old order and says so. A run that includes a new Worker refuses a `-- --secrets-file` passthrough (#2055). Test stand-in binaries are now written by a child process, which fixes intermittent `Text file busy` failures (#2064). |
| **v0.346.8** | `bynk deploy --context` now also refuses a callee whose new build no longer provides the contract a live caller was compiled against (`bynk.deploy.contract_skew`), naming each caller, the service and both hashes, before the plan and under `--dry-run`; `bynk.deploy.lock` records each Worker's `expects` beside what it provides, and a caller recorded before that is warned of, not refused (#2077). |
| **v0.346.7** | A Worker's fault handlers (`fetch`, which covers `/_bynk/call/` and `/_bynk/event/`, and the queue consumer) log the error's stack, `e instanceof Error ? (e.stack || String(e)) : e`, instead of the error as a value, so `wrangler tail` shows frames an uploaded source map can map back to `.bynk` lines; the Workers guide says so (#2053). |
| **v0.346.6** | The `dev_inspect` workerd test and the VS Code extension's two workerd debug tests stop only the `wrangler dev` process group they started, instead of running `pkill -f workerd`. That command killed every workerd on the machine, while the `wrangler` it had started (and the workerd that one respawned) leaked. `dev_inspect` also asks the OS for free ports instead of deriving them from its pid (#2069). |
| **v0.346.5** | A unit or integration test case whose agent or service handler emits now runs, and `expect Events.emit called once` observes the release (the test deps carry `__eventsDispatch`) |
| **v0.346.4** | A test now pins that every internal path a Worker answers (`/_bynk/call/`, `/_bynk/event/`) checks the caller's signature before anything else that can answer, so an unauthenticated request gets a bare `401` and never the `409` `ContractMismatch` body that names the expected hash; that body is unchanged (#2054). |
| **v0.346.3** | Under `bynk deploy --env staging`, every command a deploy message suggests repeats the environment (`bynk deploy --env staging`, `bynk deploy --env staging --force`), where a plain `bynk deploy` would have deployed the default environment, and every deploy message that names a Worker (the contract-skew refusal, edge-key and secret notices, the progress line, a failed push, the foreign caller hand-off) names the deployed `-staging` Worker, as the plan does (#2047). |
| **v0.346.2** | `bynk deploy` re-runs the cross-environment KV title check when it re-creates a namespace whose recorded id has gone from the account, and stops with the same message before `wrangler kv namespace create`, where the plan-time check could not see the re-create and wrangler failed with code 10014. At that deploy-time re-check only, another environment's recorded namespace whose id has gone no longer holds its title unless the source still declares it there. The plan-time check still counts every recorded namespace: clear a stale orphan with `bynk deploy --prune` in its own environment, or by removing it from `bynk.deploy.lock` (#2059). |
| **v0.346.1** | On `workers`, a consumed context's type on a context's own boundary (`on call(b: Batch)`) type-checks — `handlers.ts` re-exports it as `export { type Batch }` for `compose.ts`'s `handlers.Batch` and generates its codecs even when nothing calls the consumed context |
| **v0.346.0** | A literal passed straight to a cross-context call is admitted to the parameter's refined type, checked against the callee's own declaration, so `t.pay.charge(250)` for `cents: Cents` checks in a `system` case and from a consuming context, with or without `uses` of the declaring commons, instead of failing as `bynk.boundary.structural_mismatch`; a literal outside the callee's refinement is `bynk.refine.literal_violates`, and a plain `Int` or a caller's own same-named type still does not cross |
| **v0.345.2** | `bynk deploy` refuses, before the plan and under `--dry-run` too, a KV namespace it would create whose title another environment in `bynk.deploy.lock` already holds or would create (Worker `shop`'s bare namespace under `--env staging` and its `capability Staging` in the default environment are both `shop-staging`), naming both namespaces and both environments, where it compared titles within one environment only and left the clash to fail at `wrangler kv namespace create` (#2048). |
| **v0.345.1** | Under `bynk deploy --env staging`, the `--context` refusal for a binding target that has never been deployed (now naming `shop-orders-staging` and `shop-payment-staging`) and a failed run's `N further contexts were not deployed` list name each Worker as the plan does, `-staging`, where they printed the bare names; the deploy guide now says that `--format json`'s `orphans.workers` and `orphans.queues` hold account names while `orphans.kv` holds ledger keys (#2038). |
| **v0.345.0** | In a test case, a route guarded by a refinement actor takes `by Admin("root")` with the base actor's identity, and a `system` case signs the claims the refinement checks; a route guarded by an actor sum (`by who: User | Visitor`) takes any member, `by Visitor` or `by User("ada")`, and runs with that member's arm |
| **v0.344.1** | Under `bynk deploy --env <name>`, the bare `Kv` namespace is created as `<worker>-<name>` (`kv create shop-cart-staging`), where it reused the default environment's title `shop-cart`, which Cloudflare refuses as a duplicate on the same account. The default environment's titles are unchanged, and a namespace whose id `bynk.deploy.lock` already records for the environment is reused, not recreated (#2037). |
| **v0.344.0** | A `stub` in a `system`-tier case, or at the scope of a suite that runs `system` cases, is now `bynk.stub.at_system` instead of being accepted and ignored, since each context runs as the Worker it deploys as and builds its own capability providers; keep a case that needs a stubbed seam at `unit` or `integration` |
| **v0.343.3** | On Workers, a context's codec for a list, option, map or generic record of a commons type names the type unbranded (`__CommonsLine`), so a subscriber's codec for an event like `{ lines: List[Line] }`, or a caller's codec for a consumed record with such a field, accepts the other context's `Line` and passes `tsc` |
| **v0.343.2** | `bynk deploy --dry-run --env staging` names the Workers, Service Binding targets and queues a staging deploy creates (`deploy shop-orders-staging`, `order shop-payment-staging → shop-orders-staging`, `queue create job-intake-staging`), in text and in `--format json` (`order`, each context's `worker`, `binds_to` and `queues`, and orphaned queues and Workers), where it printed the bare names. The deploy itself already pushed `-<env>` Workers; it now states the Worker's `name` in the generated `[env.<env>]` block, from the same function the plan uses, rather than leaving it to Wrangler's default, which is the same name (#2028). |
| **v0.343.1** | A test case's `let` named like a service, agent or asset of the unit under test (`let level <- Counter("a").bump()` beside `service level`), or re-binding a property's `for all` binder, now shadows it in the emitted test instead of redeclaring the runner's own binding and failing `tsc` (TS2451) (#2024) |
| **v0.343.0** | A context that declares a foreign caller now gets a typed TypeScript client, `workers/<worker>/bynk-client.ts`, which a Worker that is not Bynk imports to call it: one typed function per service its actor may call (`connect({ caller: "Billing", binding: env.LEDGER, env }).lookup(id)`), the boundary types as TypeScript types, and the contract hashes and wire version embedded, so a signature change, a call the actor is not admitted to, or a runtime package for another wire version fails `tsc` in the caller's build. It signs as the declared foreign caller and encodes and decodes with the codec a Bynk caller uses, through the new runtime package `@bynk/wire-client`, which is bundled from the runtime's own boundary modules and versioned with the wire format. `bynk-contracts.json` records, under `foreign`, the hashes each foreign caller may call the context at, and the deploy record keeps them. A project pins them per caller in `bynk.foreign.toml`, and `bynk deploy` refuses a build that breaks a pin (`bynk.deploy.foreign_contract_skew`) until the pin is updated. See [TypeScript client](/book/reference/typescript-client/) and [Adopt Bynk beside a TypeScript Worker](/book/guides/projects-build-and-deployment/adopt-beside-a-typescript-worker/) |
| **v0.342.1** | `bynk.history.restates_invariant` now also catches a history property whose predicate restates a declared `transition` or `invariant` written with `is` (e.g. `run.all((s) => s.old.status is Closed implies s.new.status is Closed)`), as ADR 0155 and the testing reference already said it did (#2018) |
| **v0.342.0** | The checker refuses a `~>` send in an agent handler, which checked clean and then failed `tsc` because an agent handler's `deps` has no `__exec`: a store operation such as `~> count.update(f)` is `bynk.send.store_op`, since a store write is part of the handler's atomic commit (write `do count.update(f)`), and any other send, a capability call or another agent's handler, is `bynk.send.in_agent_handler` (await it with `let _ <- …` or `do …`) |
| **v0.341.0** | A context can admit `on call` callers that are not Bynk by declaring each as an actor with the new `Foreign` scheme, `actor Ledger { auth = Foreign(secret = "LEDGER_CALL_KEY", worker = "ledger-svc") }`, and naming it in a handler's `by` clause, alone or in a sum such as `by who: Caller | Ledger`. A foreign caller signs its calls as the actor under that secret, and is admitted only where its actor is named; `Caller` admits Bynk contexts only, and a caller a handler does not admit gets `403`. `bynk deploy` mints each foreign caller's secret, sets it on the context, and prints the `wrangler secret put` for the foreign Worker's operator. `bynk dev` and `bynk test` use a fixed local value, and a system test can call `by Ledger`. See the [wire format](/book/reference/wire-format/#foreign-callers) |
| **v0.340.1** | The language server colours a local's definition as a declaration, and find-references and rename no longer count a shadowing `let` as a use of the binding it shadows |
| **v0.340.0** | Every cross-context call and event delivery is now signed with HMAC-SHA256 under a key one caller shares with one callee (`BYNK_CALL_TO_<CALLEE>` on the caller, `BYNK_CALL_FROM_<CALLER>` on the callee), replacing the shared `BYNK_CALL_SECRET`. A Worker refuses an internal request whose signature does not verify for the caller it names (`401`), or whose body is over 1 MiB (`413`), before anything else, so a context can claim only its own name. `bynk deploy` mints one key per edge and sets it on both ends; `bynk dev` and `bynk test` use a fixed local key. The [wire format](/book/reference/wire-format/#authentication) documents the signing input. **Breaking for deployed projects:** redeploy the whole project once (`bynk deploy`, not `--context`); `BYNK_CALL_SECRET` is no longer read and can be deleted |
| **v0.339.0** | `let <pattern> = value else { … }` (and `<-` for an effect's result) binds a pattern's names for the rest of the block, and yields the `else` block, or the arms of `else match { … }`, when the value does not match, so a chain of fallible steps reads top to bottom with each failure handled beside its step |
| **v0.338.0** | The cross-context call format is published as [wire format version 1](/book/reference/wire-format/), generated from the compiler and versioned on its own, and callers now send `X-Bynk-Wire: 1`. A `/_bynk/call/` request that is not a `POST` gets `405`, malformed JSON gets `400 MalformedJson` instead of a `500`, and an unsupported `X-Bynk-Wire` gets `400 UnsupportedWireVersion`. A record field's inline refinement (`qty: Int where Positive`) is now checked when the record is decoded, at a call boundary or in an HTTP body, and is part of the contract hash; a `Duration` field must be a whole number of milliseconds, and sending a `Duration` or `Instant` outside the safe-integer range now faults in the sender (as an `Int` already did) instead of being passed on. **Breaking for deployed projects:** contracts with an inline field refinement change hash, so redeploy the whole project once. Tightening an inline field refinement later also makes stored records that break it fail to load |
| **v0.337.0** | An `as integration` case runs a consumed adapter's real binding, its own `given` capabilities taking the tier's doubles and stubs; a `suite` on an adapter runs the binding at every tier; and a case can call a capability it reaches directly (`let s <- Greeter.hello("ann")`) |
| **v0.336.0** | 'A test case''s service call is typed by its handler''s return type, so `Ok(v)` binds `v` with the handler''s result type and its methods check (#1934). A `system`-tier call with a `Wire(…)` argument, `by Nobody`, or a method with no handler yields the new `HttpOutcome[T]` (`Rejected(…)` before the handler ran, `Handled(…)` after), whose kinds are checked and whose body and path rejections carry a `detail` string. A queue verdict is matchable (`Retry(reason)`), and `bynk.test.untyped_outcome` is retired' |
| **v0.335.1** | Experimental `bynk.cloudflare` `Service` calls a Worker outside the project over a Service Binding, `capability Billing = Service @worker("legacy-billing")` (bound as `SERVICE_BILLING`); `bynk deploy` binds it without provisioning and warns when the Worker is missing from the account, and `bynk dev` binds a local stand-in unless `--local-worker legacy-billing` names it. `bynk deploy --format json` gains a per-context `foreign_services` list (additive) |
| **v0.335.0** | Every cross-context call and event delivery now carries the deployment's call secret `BYNK_CALL_SECRET` in an `X-Bynk-Call-Secret` header, and a Worker refuses a `/_bynk/call/` or `/_bynk/event/` request without it (`401`, before anything else), because those paths answered on the Worker's public `workers.dev` address and anyone could call an `on call` service claiming any caller. `bynk deploy` mints the secret and sets it on every context, `bynk dev` and `bynk test` use a fixed local value. **Breaking for deployed projects:** redeploy the whole project once (`bynk deploy`, not `--context`), since until every Worker holds the secret, calls between old and new Workers fail |
| **v0.334.0** | In a test case, a `svc.call(…)` outcome takes the handler's declared type, so `expect r == Ok("a")` checks; a `by c: Caller` handler called without `by Caller("…")` is `bynk.test.actor_identity_required`; and a handler that sends with `~>` runs at every tier, its sends awaited before the case's expectations |
| **v0.333.6** | A `fold` over a collected `store` map's `.entries` no longer names an unimported `MapEntry` type, and `.keys`/`.entries` over a map keyed by an opaque or refined type now yield that branded key type, so both pass `tsc` |
| **v0.333.5** | A SIGINT or SIGTERM sent to `bynk dev` itself now stops its `wrangler` and `workerd` processes before it exits, instead of leaving them running with their ports bound |
| **v0.333.4** | A Worker binds only the contexts it calls, so an event subscriber that `consumes` its publisher for the event type no longer forms a Service Binding cycle that `bynk deploy` refuses; a subscriber that also calls its publisher is still refused, now with the cause named |
| **v0.333.3** | "`bynk-secrets.json` no longer claims a complete read list it does not have (closes #1976): literal `Secrets.get` names in `provides` bodies — including providers reached through `given` — and in agent handlers are collected into `read`, a Worker that composes an adapter provider `given Secrets` is marked `read_complete: false` because its TypeScript binding's reads cannot be seen, and `bynk deploy`'s `secrets incomplete` line no longer blames a computed name for both" |
| **v0.333.2** | `bynk dev` with two or more contexts now starts without `--inspect`: each context gets its own inspector port instead of all racing for 9229, and a wrangler resolved via `npx` is installed once before the contexts start |
| **v0.333.1** | An agent handler that calls a consumed context's service (`Alias.service(…)`) or constructs another agent now works on the Workers target, reaching the Service Binding and the agent's namespace through the Durable Object's own `env`, instead of reading `deps.surface` and faulting with a `TypeError` |
| **v0.333.0** | A field read on a value of a consumed context's type is checked — rejected for an `exports opaque` type (`bynk.context.opaque_inspection`), typed against the providing context's declaration for an unexported one, and rejected as `bynk.boundary.ambiguous_type` where two consumed contexts declare the name differently |
| **v0.332.3** | A diagnostic's secondary label into a standard-library unit (such as `bynk.resolve.missing_field`'s "field declared here" pointing at `Request` in `bynk.bynk`) renders in a snippet of that unit's own source instead of underlining unrelated code in the user's file, and a label in another file after non-ASCII text no longer gets a wrong header or panics the renderer |
| **v0.332.2** | `bynk deploy` provisions, records and binds a KV namespace per derived capability (`capability Sessions = Kv`, titled `shop-cart-sessions`), and plans a removed or renamed instance as an orphan; `bynk dev --remote` binds every instance. **Breaking (machine output):** in `bynk deploy --format json`, a context's `kv` is now a list of `{binding, action, namespace}` (empty, not `null`, without KV), and each `orphans.kv` entry is `{worker, binding?}` rather than a bare worker name. Ledgers and configs of projects with only the bare `Kv` are unchanged |
| **v0.332.1** | An increment acknowledges a gated greenfield-status probe it moves in its pending file's `probes` key, and the merge-time stamp rewrites `design/greenfield-status.md`, so parallel PRs no longer conflict on the table |
| **v0.332.0** | A derived capability, `capability Sessions = Kv`, is a distinct instance of a platform resource with its own Worker binding (`KV_SESSIONS`); `bynk deploy` does not provision instances yet |
| **v0.331.0** | Standard-library items are stable or experimental, with an `[unstable] allow` opt-in in `bynk.toml`; a `match` on the extensible `FetchError` or `Method` needs a wildcard |
| **v0.330.16** | A `system`-tier suite stands up every subscriber to a participant's events and gives each participant its own in-memory `Kv` namespace per case, so an emitted event is delivered and a `Kv` call works instead of faulting |
| **v0.330.15** | Every place the compiler reads a Worker binding by name now gets the name from the binding's constructor, and a test pins `bynk.cloudflare`'s `env.KV` read to `KV_BINDING_NAME` |
| **v0.330.14** | A handler's Workers `deps.env` type is built from the Worker's binding list and names only consumed contexts with services, so a bare `consumes bynk` beside a cross-context call no longer fails `tsc --strict` |
| **v0.330.13** | 'A value of a type a consumed context does not export can now be passed back to that context: `let b <- shop.quote()` then `shop.take(b)` was rejected as a `bynk.boundary.structural_mismatch` against its own declaration. A service or capability return that the caller''s own type of the same name cannot hold, because the shapes differ, is now rejected at the call with `bynk.boundary.structural_mismatch`, where before the caller''s type silently typed the value and its fields (#1941)' |
| **v0.330.12** | First-party units are injected from a registry in `bynk-std`, following each unit's own `uses`/`consumes`; emitted output is unchanged |
| **v0.330.11** | A Worker's `Env` interface now declares exactly the bindings in its `wrangler.toml`, including the Service Bindings to its event subscribers |
| **v0.330.10** | 'On the `workers` target, a context calling a service whose signature carries a `commons` type the caller does not `uses`, or a type the providing context does not export, now compiles: the caller imports the commons'' codecs or generates its own under the names the call uses. Both failed `tsc` before, though the checker and the `bundle` target accepted them (#1932)' |
| **v0.330.9** | The first-party standard-library sources move into a new data-only `bynk-std` crate; emitted output is unchanged |
| **v0.330.8** | A Workers build reports two bindings with one name in a Worker's `wrangler.toml`, such as an agent `Kv` and the `bynk.cloudflare` KV namespace (both `KV`), as `bynk.context.duplicate_binding` |
| **v0.330.7** | 'A method on an asset''s `url` written on the chain (`sheet.url.startsWith("/")`) checks in a context and its test suites, as it already did in a commons. It was misread as a call into a context named `sheet.url` (`bynk.resolve.unconsumed_context`) (#1930)' |
| **v0.330.6** | 'In a test case, matching or testing a service call''s outcome binds the fixed type of a variant''s payload (`Raw(body, contentType)` binds `Bytes` and `String`, a message or redirect variant `String`), so its methods work. The match''s arms are now checked, and the match has their type when they agree, and a method on a payload of the handler''s result type is `bynk.test.untyped_outcome` instead of TypeScript that failed `tsc` (#1931)' |
| **v0.330.5** | 'A local function, asset, service, agent or provider named like a type the unit imports with `uses` hides that type: writing the type''s name, or constructing one of its variants bare, is `bynk.uses.name_conflict`, while its values still flow through; one named like a type a consumed context exports is `bynk.consumes.name_conflict`. The checker accepted both, and the emitted TypeScript failed `tsc` (#1927)' |
| **v0.330.4** | A diagnostic's secondary label in another file of a multi-file unit (a duplicate's "previously declared here") renders in a snippet of that file in `bynkc`, and points at that file's URI in the language server's `relatedInformation`, instead of being drawn at the same offsets in the diagnostic's own file |
| **v0.330.3** | 'A service, agent or bodied provider can no longer share its name with a function, asset, type, event, or each other in the same unit (`bynk.resolve.name_conflict`), and a local declaration of any kind now shadows a `uses`-imported function or asset of its name, with a reference it cannot answer reported as `bynk.uses.name_conflict`: the checker accepted both, and the emitted TypeScript failed `tsc` (#1925)' |
| **v0.330.2** | 'Editor tooling for asset names: hover shows the declaration, where the platform serves the file (`/_assets/<stem>.<hash>.<ext>` or its `at` path) and its doc; completion offers asset names wherever they are in scope (a unit''s own and its `uses`d commons'') and `url` after one; go-to-definition, references, rename, doc links and the add-`uses` quick fix reach them; and asset names get their own `asset` semantic token (static assets)' |
| **v0.330.1** | 'An asset fetch in a suite (`api.GET(style)`) is target-scoped: only the target''s own assets are nameable, it works through a `from http` service only, and it is its own callee, so no route can be mistaken for it (static assets, review of slice 3)' |
| **v0.330.0** | 'A suite names its target''s assets and fetches one with `api.GET(style)`, answered as the platform''s asset tier would (`Raw(body, contentType)`, or `NotFound`); `bynk.test.asset_address_method`, `bynk.test.principal_on_asset`; a case now binds an `HttpResult` variant''s payload by its real field (`Raw(body, ct)`, `BadRequest(m)`) (static assets, slice 3)' |
| **v0.329.0** | 'An `asset` may be served at a fixed path (`asset favicon = "assets/favicon.ico" at "/favicon.ico"`), checked against the Worker''s routes (`bynk.asset.shadows_route`, the warning `bynk.asset.may_shadow_route`, `bynk.asset.path_conflict`, `bynk.asset.invalid_at_path`); `/_assets/` is a reserved route prefix; a Worker ships the hashed assets of the contexts it consumes (static assets, slice 2)' |
| **v0.328.1** | 'A build reads only the `assets/` files an `asset` declaration names, so large undeclared media there no longer slow `bynkc compile` or each `bynk dev` rebuild; a declared file that cannot be read is the new `bynk.asset.unreadable_file` (static assets, #1883)' |
| **v0.328.0** | '`asset` declarations build: each Worker serves its files from `assets/` at a content-hashed `.url`, through an `[assets]` block and an immutable `_headers`; new `bynk.asset.outside_assets_dir`, `bynk.asset.missing_file`, `bynk.asset.needs_project`, and the `bynk.asset.not_served` warning for a deployable Bundle build (static assets, slice 1b)' |
| **v0.327.0** | A context or commons declares a static file with `asset name = "path"`, a value of the new built-in type `Asset` whose `.url` is a `String`; declarations are checked, and a build refuses them with `bynk.asset.not_yet_emitted` until they can be emitted (static assets, slice 1a) |
| **v0.326.2** | Build output can carry binary static assets, written byte-for-byte and pruned from a Worker's compiler-owned assets directory (static assets, slice 0) |
| **v0.326.1** | "**A nested list type is valid TypeScript** ([#1898](https://github.com/accuser/bynk/issues/1898)). `List[List[Int]]` now emits `readonly (readonly number[])[]` instead of `readonly readonly number[][]`, which `tsc` rejected (TS1354); a function-typed element (`List[Int -> Int]`) is parenthesised the same way. Covers both declared types and the List/Query kernel lowerings' inline annotations." |
| **v0.326.0** | "One-line docs and member docs (#1888). A `--|` line that is the first thing on its line is a doc line; consecutive doc lines form one doc, and a bare `--|` separates paragraphs. Record fields, sum variants (pipe and `enum` form), variant payload fields and `messages` entries now take a doc in either form, a `--|` run or a `---` block; hover on a field (at its declaration, an access or a record-literal label) and on a variant shows it, completion items carry it, and the documentation page lists documented members under their type. A target documented in both forms is the new error `bynk.parse.doc_forms_mixed`. `bynk fmt` keeps each doc in the form it was written in. The tree-sitter grammar gains a `doc_line` token (highlighted as documentation), the TextMate grammar a `--|` rule, VS Code continues `--| ` on Enter, and the language server classifies doc lines as `comment` + `documentation` semantic tokens" |
| **v0.325.0** | "`Fetch.send` sends extra request headers and returns the response's: `Request` and `Response` gain `headers: Map[String, String]` (a required field — existing literals add `headers: Map.empty()`), and `FetchError` gains `InvalidHeader` for a request whose headers conflict with a typed slot, name a header the platform owns or are not legal headers, or whose `contentType`/`authorization` value is not a legal header value (#1886)" |
| **v0.324.0** | "**Module docs** ([#1885](https://github.com/accuser/bynk/issues/1885)). A doc-block above a `commons`/`context`/`adapter` header is the unit's module doc. A unit split across files carries at most one: two or more is `bynk.project.duplicate_module_doc`, naming every file that carries one. Hover on a unit name (its header, a `uses` target, a `consumes` target) shows the unit's module doc, found in whichever of its files has it, and the documentation page merges every file of a multi-file unit under that doc. New guide: [Document your code](/book/guides/program-structure/document-your-code/); spec [§5.1a](/book/spec/static-semantics/#module-docs)." |
| **v0.323.1** | "`bynk fmt` keeps the blank line between a standalone comment and the next top-level declaration, so the comment no longer reads as describing it (#1884)" |
